## 5. Named types and methods

`type({ description, shape, display, methods? })` declares a **named type**: a shape with a name (`notes/Note`), shown to
people in a consistent way, that other systems can use as a field and that `@` mentions can point at.

_From `scripts/docs/example/shared/index.ts`:_

```ts
/** A note: a titled text with tags. A named type, so other systems can use it as a field and mentions show it. */
export const Note = type({
  description: 'A note: a titled text with tags.',
  shape: g.obj({
    id: g.text({ minLength: 1 }),
    title: g.text({ minLength: 1 }),
    text: hub.types.LongText,
    tags: g.list(g.text()),
    pinned: g.bool(),
    updatedAt: g.timestamp(),
  }),
  // chips and cards name a note by its title, with its id under it
  display: { label: 'Note', plural: 'Notes', icon: 'note', color: '#d9a400', title: 'title', subtitle: 'id' },
  methods: {
    // offered on a note's mention card; people and code can call it, the LLM can't (`visibility: 'user'`)
    pin: fn({ description: 'Pin or unpin this note.', args: g.obj({ pinned: g.bool() }), returns: g.bool(), visibility: 'user' }),
  },
});
```

- **`shape`** is any gin type; `ValueOf<typeof Note>` (or `Infer<typeof Note.shape>`) is its value type.
- **`display`**: `label` and `plural` name the type for people, `icon` and `color` mark chips and cards, and `title` and
  `subtitle` name the fields that name a value. `title` and `subtitle` must be text or number fields of the shape; a
  name that isn't one doesn't compile.
- **`methods`** are native fns called on a value of the type. Declare each with `fn()`; its qualified name is
  `notes/Note.pin`, and its args are the value as `self` plus its own args (`{ self: Note, pinned: boolean }`). A person
  can run a method from a mention's card (when the mention provider declares `of: types.Note`), code calls it through
  `notes.types.Note.methods.pin` as a dep, and the LLM sees it only when its `visibility` says so (`'user'` hides it).

The server implements every method under `types`, getting the value as `self`:

_From `scripts/docs/example/server/impl.ts`:_

```ts
types: {
  Note: {
    pin: {
      async run(self, args, ctx) {
        const store = deps.open(ctx.dataDir);
        const note = await store.get(self.id);
        if (note === undefined) { return ctx.invalid([{ path: 'self', message: `Note ${self.id} no longer exists.` }]); }
        await store.put({ ...note, pinned: args.pinned, updatedAt: deps.now() });
        return ctx.done(args.pinned);
      },
    },
  },
},
```

Use a named type for things with identity that people point at or other systems take as input (a file entry, a row, a
ticket). For a one-off shape, a plain `g.obj` constant is enough.

### Reference: named types and methods

#### `type`

function · `import { type } from '@harness/sdk'`

Declare a named type. Methods are native fns called on a value of the type: a person can run them from a mention's
card, and code can call them through the type's ref. They are offered to the LLM only when their `visibility`
says so.

```ts
function type<T, const M extends Methods = {}>(def: { description: string; shape: GinType<T>; display: Omit<TypeDisplay, "title" | "subtitle"> & { title?: NamingKeys<T>; subtitle?: NamingKeys<T>; }; methods?: M; }): TypeDef<T, M>;
```

Refers to: [`TypeDisplay`](#core-typedisplay).

#### `TypeDef`

interface · `import { TypeDef } from '@harness/sdk'`

A named type in a contract: its shape, how it is shown, and its methods.

```ts
interface TypeDef<T, M extends Methods> {
  readonly description: string;
  readonly shape: GinType<T>;
  readonly display: TypeDisplay;
  readonly methods: M;
}
```

Refers to: [`TypeDisplay`](#core-typedisplay).

#### `AnyTypeDef`

type · `import { AnyTypeDef } from '@harness/sdk'`

```ts
type AnyTypeDef = TypeDef<unknown, Methods>;
```

#### `ValueOf`

type · `import { ValueOf } from '@harness/sdk'`

The value type of a declared type.

```ts
type ValueOf<D> = D extends TypeDef<infer T, Methods> ? T : never;
```

#### `NamingKeys`

type · `import { NamingKeys } from '@harness/sdk'`

Fields of T that can name a value (text or number fields).

```ts
type NamingKeys<T> = { [K in keyof T]-?: T[K] extends string | number | undefined ? K : never }[keyof T] & string;
```

#### `TypeRef`

type · `import { TypeRef } from '@harness/sdk'`

A typed handle to a named type (`fs.types.Entry`). It is a real gin type itself (a named gin extension, registered
in `g`), so other types can use it as a field: `g.obj({ entry: fs.types.Entry })`. Its methods are typed fn refs
that code calls with the value as `self`.

```ts
type TypeRef<N extends QualifiedName, T, M extends Methods = {}> = Extension<T> & {
  readonly ref: 'type';
  readonly name: N;
  readonly display: TypeDisplay;
  readonly description: string;
  readonly methods: MethodRefs<N, T, M>;
};
```

Refers to: [`QualifiedName`](#core-qualifiedname), [`TypeDisplay`](#core-typedisplay).

#### `AnyTypeRef`

type · `import { AnyTypeRef } from '@harness/sdk'`

```ts
type AnyTypeRef = TypeRef<QualifiedName, unknown, Methods>;
```

Refers to: [`QualifiedName`](#core-qualifiedname).

#### `Methods`

type · `import { Methods } from '@harness/sdk'`

A type's methods by name. Each is declared with `fn()` and implemented with the value it is called on.

```ts
type Methods = Record<string, FnDef<FnTypes>>;
```

#### `MethodName`

type · `import { MethodName } from '@harness/sdk'`

A method's qualified name: `fs/Entry.reveal`.

```ts
type MethodName<N extends QualifiedName, K> = N extends `${infer S}/${infer L}` ? QualifiedName<S, `${L}.${K & string}`> : never;
```

Refers to: [`QualifiedName`](#core-qualifiedname).

#### `MethodArgs`

type · `import { MethodArgs } from '@harness/sdk'`

The args of a method call: the value it is called on, as `self`, plus the method's own args.

```ts
type MethodArgs<T, A> = Simplify<{ self: T } & A>;
```

#### `MethodRefs`

type · `import { MethodRefs } from '@harness/sdk'`

The fn refs of a type's methods.

```ts
type MethodRefs<N extends QualifiedName, T, M extends Methods> = {
  readonly [K in keyof M]: M[K] extends FnDef<infer F> ? FnRef<MethodName<N, K>, MethodArgs<T, F['args']>, F['result'], F['handlers']> : never;
};
```

Refers to: [`QualifiedName`](#core-qualifiedname).

#### `MethodImpl`

type · `import { MethodImpl } from '@harness/sdk'`

A method's implementation: a native fn that also gets the value it was called on.

```ts
type MethodImpl<V, T extends FnTypes, Cfg = unknown> =
{ run(self: V, args: T['args'], ctx: FnCtx<T, Cfg>): Promise<Outcome<T>> } &
([T['undo']] extends [never] ? { undo?: never } : { undo(payload: T['undo'], ctx: UndoCtx<Cfg>): Promise<UndoOutcome> });
```

Refers to: [`UndoOutcome`](#core-undooutcome).

## 6. Fns

A **fn** is something people, agents and code can run: a name, a description, typed args and a typed result. A
**native** fn is implemented in code by its own system and declared with `fn({ … })`. Only `description`, `args` and
`returns` are required; every other option changes what the fn can do, and the types follow:

| Option | What it does | What it changes in the server |
|---|---|---|
| `description` | one sentence: what it does, for people and the LLM | |
| `instructions` | extra guidance the LLM sees when the fn is in its toolset | |
| `args`, `returns` | gin types of the args (an object) and the result | `run(args, ctx)` gets typed args; `ctx.done(result)` checks the result |
| `examples` | example calls (`{ args, note? }`) agents see on its card and when they get its args wrong | a wrong example doesn't compile |
| `status` | the words its runs show, per phase (below) | tokens are checked against args and result |
| `tags` | free labels; `read` marks a fn that changes nothing, so policies may run it without asking | |
| `deps` | fns its implementation may call, as refs: `{ read: fs.fns.read }` | `ctx.fns.read(args)` exists, typed; nothing else can be called |
| `handlers` | callbacks the caller may supply mid-run (**Handlers and progress**) | `ctx.handlers.confirm(...)` |
| `progress` | `linear` or `steps({ … })` (**Handlers and progress**) | `ctx.progress.set(f)` or `ctx.progress.step(name, status)` |
| `undo` | a gin type of the undo payload: the fn is undoable | `undo(payload, ctx)` must be implemented |
| `resume` | a gin type of the resume payload: the fn can pause | `ctx.pause(state)`, `ctx.onPause`, `ctx.resume` |
| `state` | the session state fields it reads and writes (**Session state, forms and display**) | `ctx.state` sees exactly these fields |
| `form` | a gin type its renderer submits back to the waiting run | `await ctx.form()` |
| `display` | how a finished run looks: `card` (default), `message`, `hidden` | |
| `operations` | `{ cancel?, background? }`, both default `true`; pause and undo follow `resume` and `undo` | |
| `limits` | `{ timeout?, retries?, cost? }`: hard limit per run, retries for `retry` failures, a rough cost | |
| `visibility` | who sees it: `both` (default), `user` (people only, not the LLM), `llm`, `none` | |

_From `scripts/docs/example/shared/index.ts`:_

```ts
add: fn({
  description: 'Add a note.',
  instructions: 'Give it a short title. Tags are lowercase words; the config adds its default tags.',
  args: g.obj({
    title: g.text({ minLength: 1 }),
    text: hub.types.LongText,
    tags: g.optional(g.list(g.text())),
  }),
  returns: Note.shape,
  // example calls agents see on its card and when they get its args wrong; a wrong example doesn't compile
  examples: [{ args: { title: 'Groceries', text: 'Milk, eggs, bread.', tags: ['home'] }, note: 'A short title and lowercase tags.' }],
  // declaring an undo type makes it undoable, and the server must implement `undo`
  undo: Before,
  status: {
    active: { one: 'Adding {args.title}…', many: 'Adding {n} notes…' },
    retry: { one: 'Retrying adding {args.title}…', many: 'Retrying {n} notes…' },
    done: { one: 'Added {output.title}', many: 'Added {n} notes' },
    failed: { one: 'Adding {args.title} failed', many: '{n} notes failed' },
  },
}),
```

_From `scripts/docs/example/shared/index.ts`:_

```ts
find: fn({
  description: 'Find notes by words in their title or text, or by tag.',
  args: g.obj({
    query: g.optional(g.text()),
    tag: g.optional(g.text()),
    limit: g.optional(g.num({ min: 1, max: 50, whole: true })),
  }),
  returns: g.obj({ notes: g.list(Note.shape), total: g.num({ min: 0, whole: true }) }),
  // reads and writes `lastQuery` in the session's state; only fns that declare it see it
  state: LastSearch,
  progress: linear,
  // a read fn: it changes nothing, so policies can let it run without asking
  tags: ['read'],
  examples: [{ args: { query: 'release plan' } }, { args: { tag: 'work', limit: 5 }, note: 'The five newest notes tagged work.' }],
}),
```

**Status words.** `status` gives each phase (`active`, `retry`, `done`, `failed`) a `one` form for a single run and a
`many` form for a group of runs of the fn. In `one` forms, `{args.<path>}` reads the args and, in `done` only,
`{output.<path>}` reads the result; `many` forms may use only `{fn}` and `{n}` (the count). `{args.path|a file}`
gives a fallback for a missing value. A token for a path the args or result don't have doesn't compile. Without
`status`, runs show the kind's words or the hub's defaults (`DEFAULT_STATUS`). Give all four phases; keep them short
and in the present (`Reading {args.path}…`) and past (`Read {args.path}`) tense.

**Writing fns an LLM uses well.** The description says what it does in one sentence. Args are few, named for what
they are, with `g.field(…, { docs })` where a value needs explaining; prefer enums and bounded numbers to free text.
Return what the caller needs next (ids, counts, whether it was truncated), not a wall of text. Use `examples` for any
fn whose args are easy to get wrong. Tag read-only fns `read`.

**Implementing a fn.** Each native fn is `{ async run(args, ctx) { … } }` under `fns` in the implementation, returning an
outcome built with the ctx helpers (**Outcomes**):

_From `scripts/docs/example/server/impl.ts`:_

```ts
add: {
  async run(args, ctx) {
    const store = deps.open(ctx.dataDir);
    const note = await create(store, ctx.config, { title: args.title, text: args.text, tags: args.tags ?? [] });
    // a real refusal, not a mistake in the args: `fail` with the `caller` class and a stable code
    if (note === undefined) { return ctx.fail('caller', full(ctx.config), { code: 'full' }); }
    ctx.attach(notes.attachments.note, note, `Added ${note.title}`);
    // the undo payload is typed by the contract's `undo` type
    return ctx.done(note, { undo: { id: note.id, note: null } });
  },
  undo: (payload, ctx) => restore(deps.open(ctx.dataDir), payload),
},
```

Fns can also be added and removed while the system runs: `running.add(name, fn({ … }), impl)` returns the new fn's
typed ref, and `running.remove(ref)` removes it (**The server**).

**Validation hooks.** `validate: { input, output }` adds checks the hub asks the system for around a run. `input(ctx,
args, state)` runs before the run starts: `{ ok: false, reason }` means the fn can't run now, and the model reads "Not
available now: reason" (it isn't an error, and the fn isn't run). `output(ctx, args, result, state)` runs after it is
done: `{ ok: false, action: 'retry', reason }` asks the model to try again (a few times a turn), `action: 'stop'` fails
the run with the reason shown to the person. `ctx` is the system context with the run and, for output, the attempt;
`state` is the session state fields the fn declares, as they are now. An output check runs after the fn has done its
work, and a refused result's undo is dropped: a fn with side effects should check what it can in `input`, or undo its
own work before answering `retry` or `stop`.

**Per-person systems.** A system that acts as each person (a calendar) adds `connect` to its contract with
`.with({ connect: { title, description, how, settings, fns } })`, naming the fns that act as the person and whether
each is on and asks first by default. Its implementation then has `connect: { start, finish, disconnect }`: `start`
gives the provider's sign-in URL (pass the hub's `state` through), `finish` turns what came back into the account and
credentials (or a `problem`), `disconnect` revokes them. In a run, `ctx.connection` is the connection it acts as
(credentials and the person's settings), and `ctx.updateConnection({ credentials })` hands refreshed tokens back.
A fn that adds a fn for the person (a saved form) gives it to them with `ctx.shareFn(name, { visibility, with })`.

```ts
buy: fn({
  description: 'Buy an item.', args: g.obj({ count: g.num({ whole: true }) }), returns: g.obj({ total: g.num() }),
  validate: {
    input: (_ctx, args) => (args.count > 0 ? { ok: true } : { ok: false, reason: 'Buy at least one.' }),
    output: (ctx, _args, result) => (result.total <= 100 ? { ok: true } : { ok: false, action: ctx.attempt > 1 ? 'stop' : 'retry', reason: 'Too expensive.' }),
  },
}),
```

**Approval.** `approval: {}` makes every run of a fn wait for the person's approval, whatever the policies say (use it
for writes and schema changes). By default (`preview: true`) the hub first asks the implementation's
`preview(args, ctx)` what the run would do and shows it on the approval card: return `{ preview: { title, parts,
irreversible } }` (parts: `text`, `code`, `table`, `type-diff`), or `{ refused: 'why' }` to fail the run as invalid
input. A preview gets a `PreviewCtx` (the system context, the waiting run and its person) and must not change
anything. `remember: false` (the default) means the person can't say "don't ask again". A kind does the same from
`describe` (`approval`) and `preview(payload, args, ctx)`. Serving a fn that asks for a preview without implementing
one throws.

### Reference: fns

#### `fn`

function · `import { fn } from '@harness/sdk'`

Declare a native fn: a fn its system implements in code.

```ts
function fn<A, R, const D extends Deps = {}, const H extends Handlers = {}, const P extends ProgressDef = NoProgress, S = never, U = never, St extends object = {}, F = never, const T extends StatusTemplates = { active: { one: string; many: string; }; retry: { one: string; many: string; }; done: { one: string; many: string; }; failed: { one: string; many: string; }; }>(def: FnInput<A, R, D, H, P, S, U, St, F, T>): FnDef<NativeFnTypes<A, R, NoInfer<D>, NoInfer<H>, NoInfer<P>, NoInfer<S>, NoInfer<U>, NoInfer<St>, NoInfer<F>>>;
```

#### `FnInput`

interface · `import { FnInput } from '@harness/sdk'`

What `fn()` takes. Only `description`, `args` and `returns` are required.

```ts
interface FnInput<A, R, D extends Deps, H extends Handlers, P extends ProgressDef, S, U, St extends object, F, T extends StatusTemplates = StatusTemplates> {
  description: string;
  /** Extra guidance the LLM sees when the fn is in its toolset. */
  instructions?: string;
  args: GinType<A>;
  returns: GinType<R>;
  /** Fns the implementation may call. Nothing else can be called. */
  deps?: D;
  /** Callbacks the caller may supply. */
  handlers?: H;
  progress?: P;
  /**
   * What its runs say they are doing, per phase, for one run and for a group: `{ active: { one: 'Reading
   * {args.path}…', many: 'Reading {n} files…' }, … }`. Tokens are checked against its args and result.
   */
  status?: StatusInput<NoInfer<A>, NoInfer<R>, T>;
  /** Declaring a resume type makes the fn pausable. */
  resume?: GinType<S>;
  /** Declaring an undo type makes the fn undoable, and requires an `undo` implementation. */
  undo?: GinType<U>;
  /**
   * The session state fields this fn uses: `g.obj({ cwd: g.text() })`. A fn sees only the fields it declares, and a
   * field is shared only with fns that declare the same name with the same type.
   */
  state?: GinType<St>;
  /** Declaring a form type lets the run wait for its renderer to submit one (`await ctx.form()`). */
  form?: GinType<F>;
  /** Default `card`. */
  display?: Display;
  /** Defaults: cancel yes, background yes. Pause and undo follow `resume` and `undo`. */
  operations?: Partial<Pick<Operations, 'cancel' | 'background'>>;
  limits?: FnLimits;
  /** Default `both`. */
  visibility?: Audience;
  tags?: string[];
  /**
   * Example calls (args of its own args type, optionally the result of its returns type, and a note), shown to agents
   * on its card and when they get its args wrong. Give real, varied ones: they teach a model more than the description.
   */
  examples?: FnExampleOf<NoInfer<A>, NoInfer<R>>[];
  /**
   * Checks the hub runs before a run starts (`input`) and after it is done (`output`):
   * `{ input: (ctx, args, state) => args.path === '' ? { ok: false, reason: 'No file is open.' } : { ok: true } }`.
   */
  validate?: FnValidators<NoInfer<A>, NoInfer<R>, NoInfer<St>>;
  /**
   * Every run waits for the person's approval, whatever the policies say (writes, schema changes). `preview`
   * (default true) has the hub ask the implementation's `preview(args, ctx)` what the run would do first;
   * `remember` (default false) lets the person say "don't ask again".
   */
  approval?: { preview?: boolean; remember?: boolean };
  /** For a fleet: always one instance, or whichever is first available (never refused as ambiguous). */
  routing?: FnRouting;
  /**
   * What it does to data: its mode (`read`, `write`, `delete`), the named types it reads and writes, and whether its own
   * work is free (reading a file). The hub works out the rest and folds in what it uses (`FnFacts`).
   */
  effects?: FnEffects;
  /** How many of its examples go into the model's prompt with it (the hub's default when absent); the rest page with `hub/fn_examples`. */
  promptExamples?: number;
}
```

Refers to: [`Audience`](#core-audience), [`Display`](#core-display), [`FnEffects`](#core-fneffects), [`FnLimits`](#core-fnlimits), [`FnRouting`](#core-fnrouting), [`Operations`](#core-operations).

#### `FnExampleOf`

interface · `import { FnExampleOf } from '@harness/sdk'`

One example call of a fn, shown on its card at `full` detail, in its invalid-input replies and by `hub/fn_examples`:
args of the fn's own args type (a wrong example does not compile), optionally the result it gave (of its returns
type), and what the example shows. The hub keeps every example in its own table, embedded, so the ones closest to
the turn go into the prompt with the fn.

```ts
interface FnExampleOf<A, R = unknown> {
  readonly args: A;
  readonly result?: R;
  readonly note?: string;
}
```

#### `FnDef`

interface · `import { FnDef } from '@harness/sdk'`

A fn's contract as declared in `shared/`. Made with `fn()`, `agent()` or `kinds.x.fn()`.

```ts
interface FnDef<T extends FnTypes> {
  readonly kind: T['kind'];
  readonly payload: T['payload'];
  readonly description: string;
  readonly instructions: string | undefined;
  readonly args: GinType<T['args']>;
  readonly returns: GinType<T['result']>;
  readonly deps: T['deps'];
  readonly handlers: T['handlers'];
  readonly progress: T['progress'];
  /** What its runs say they are doing; its kind's words, then the hub's, when undefined. */
  readonly status: StatusTemplates | undefined;
  readonly resume: GinType<T['resume']> | undefined;
  readonly undo: GinType<T['undo']> | undefined;
  readonly state: GinType<T['state']> | undefined;
  readonly form: GinType<T['form']> | undefined;
  /** How a run looks in the chat once it is done. */
  readonly display: Display;
  readonly operations: Operations;
  readonly limits: FnLimits;
  readonly visibility: Audience;
  readonly tags: readonly string[];
  /** Example calls, checked against its args type. */
  readonly examples: readonly FnExampleOf<T['args'], T['result']>[];
  /** Its validation hooks, run in its system before and after a run. */
  readonly validate: FnValidators<T['args'], T['result'], T['state']> | undefined;
  /** Every run waits for approval; with `preview`, its implementation's `preview` says what the run would do. */
  readonly approval: FnApproval | undefined;
  /** Which instance of a fleet runs it: always one (`{ to: 'instance', instance }`), or the first available (`{ to: 'any' }`). */
  readonly routing: FnRouting | undefined;
  /** What it does to data, as declared. */
  readonly effects: FnEffects | undefined;
  /** How many examples go into the prompt with it. */
  readonly promptExamples: number | undefined;
  /** Phantom: lets the SDK read T back out of a def. Never set at runtime. */
  readonly types?: T;
}
```

Refers to: [`Audience`](#core-audience), [`Display`](#core-display), [`FnApproval`](#core-fnapproval), [`FnEffects`](#core-fneffects), [`FnLimits`](#core-fnlimits), [`FnRouting`](#core-fnrouting), [`Operations`](#core-operations).

#### `AnyFnDef`

type · `import { AnyFnDef } from '@harness/sdk'`

```ts
type AnyFnDef = FnDef<FnTypes>;
```

#### `FnTypes`

interface · `import { FnTypes } from '@harness/sdk'`

Everything the type system knows about one fn. A fn's def, its ref, its context and its renderer props are all
computed from this, so they can't disagree.

```ts
interface FnTypes {
  /** `native` (implemented in code by its own system) or the ref of the kind that runs it. */
  kind: 'native' | AnyKindRef;
  /** The kind's payload; `undefined` for native fns. */
  payload: unknown;
  args: unknown;
  result: unknown;
  deps: Deps;
  handlers: Handlers;
  progress: ProgressDef;
  /** The resume payload type; `never` when the fn can't pause. */
  resume: unknown;
  /** The undo payload type; `never` when the fn can't be undone. */
  undo: unknown;
  /** The fields of its system's session state this fn reads and writes; `{}` when none. */
  state: object;
  /** What its renderer's form submits back to the run; `never` when it has no form. */
  form: unknown;
}
```

#### `NativeFnTypes`

interface · `import { NativeFnTypes } from '@harness/sdk'`

The types of a native fn.

```ts
interface NativeFnTypes<A, R, D extends Deps, H extends Handlers, P extends ProgressDef, S, U, St extends object = {}, F = never> {
  kind: 'native'; payload: undefined; args: A; result: R; deps: D; handlers: H; progress: P; resume: S; undo: U; state: St; form: F;
}
```

#### `TypesOf`

type · `import { TypesOf } from '@harness/sdk'`

The types of a fn def.

```ts
type TypesOf<F> = F extends FnDef<infer T> ? T : never;
```

#### `Deps`

type · `import { Deps } from '@harness/sdk'`

A fn's deps by local name: `{ stat: fs.fns.stat }`.

```ts
type Deps = Record<string, AnyFnRef>;
```

#### `FnRef`

interface · `import { FnRef } from '@harness/sdk'`

A typed handle to a fn: declare it as a dep, then call it with checked args, result and handlers.

```ts
interface FnRef<N extends QualifiedName, A, R, H extends Handlers = {}> {
  readonly ref: 'fn';
  readonly name: N;
  readonly args: GinType<A>;
  readonly returns: GinType<R>;
  readonly handlers: H;
}
```

Refers to: [`QualifiedName`](#core-qualifiedname).

#### `AnyFnRef`

type · `import { AnyFnRef } from '@harness/sdk'`

```ts
type AnyFnRef = FnRef<QualifiedName, unknown, unknown, Handlers>;
```

Refers to: [`QualifiedName`](#core-qualifiedname).

#### `FnValidators`

interface · `import { FnValidators } from '@harness/sdk'`

A fn's validation hooks, run in its system when the hub asks (`validate` on the system link). `input` runs before
a run starts: a refusal tells the caller the fn can't run now and why (it is not an error). `output` runs after a
run is done: `retry` gives the model the reason and asks it to try again; `stop` fails the run with the reason
shown to the person. Both get the session state fields the fn declares, as they are now.

```ts
interface FnValidators<A, R, St extends object> {
  input?(ctx: ValidateCtx, args: A, state: Readonly<Partial<St>>): InputVerdict | Promise<InputVerdict>;
  output?(ctx: ValidateCtx, args: A, result: R, state: Readonly<Partial<St>>): OutputVerdict | Promise<OutputVerdict>;
}
```

Refers to: [`InputVerdict`](#core-inputverdict), [`OutputVerdict`](#core-outputverdict).

#### `ValidateCtx`

interface · `import { ValidateCtx } from '@harness/sdk'`

What a fn's validation hooks get (`fn({ validate })`): the system's context, the run being checked (who it is for,
its session and time), and for an output check which attempt this is (1 for the first result).

```ts
interface ValidateCtx<Cfg = unknown> extends SystemCtx<Cfg> {
  readonly run: RunInfo;
  /** The attempt whose result is checked; 1 for an input check. */
  readonly attempt: number;
}
```

Refers to: [`RunInfo`](#core-runinfo).

#### `PreviewCtx`

interface · `import { PreviewCtx } from '@harness/sdk'`

What a `preview` gets: the system context, the run waiting for approval and whom it is for. A preview must not
change anything: it reads what the run would touch and says so.

```ts
interface PreviewCtx<Cfg = unknown> extends SystemCtx<Cfg> {
  readonly run: RunId;
  readonly user: UserInfo;
}
```

Refers to: [`RunId`](#core-runid), [`UserInfo`](#core-userinfo).

#### `DEFAULT_STATUS`

const · `import { DEFAULT_STATUS } from '@harness/sdk'`

The words every fn falls back to. They use only `{fn}` and `{n}`, so they always resolve.

```ts
const DEFAULT_STATUS = {
  active: { one: '{fn}…', many: '{fn} ×{n}…' },
  retry: { one: 'Retrying {fn}…', many: 'Retrying {fn} ×{n}…' },
  done: { one: '{fn} done', many: '{fn} ×{n} done' },
  failed: { one: '{fn} failed', many: '{fn} ×{n} failed' },
} as const satisfies StatusTemplates;
```

#### `StatusTemplates`

const, type · `import { StatusTemplates } from '@harness/sdk'`

A fn's (or a kind's) status templates, one per phase.

```ts
type StatusTemplates = {
  active: {
    one: string;
    many: string;
  };
  retry: {
    one: string;
    many: string;
  };
  done: {
    one: string;
    many: string;
  };
  failed: {
    one: string;
    many: string;
  };
};
```

#### `StatusForms`

const, type · `import { StatusForms } from '@harness/sdk'`

One phase's words: `one` for a single run ("Reading {args.path}…"), `many` for a group ("Reading {n} files…").

```ts
type StatusForms = {
  one: string;
  many: string;
};
```

#### `RunPhase`

const, type · `import { RunPhase } from '@harness/sdk'`

Status words: core's templates, typed against a fn's args and result.

```ts
type RunPhase = "done" | "failed" | "active" | "retry";
```

#### `StatusInput`

type · `import { StatusInput } from '@harness/sdk'`

What a fn's `status` option takes: the templates as written, checked against its args and result.

```ts
type StatusInput<A, R, T extends StatusTemplates> = T & CheckedStatus<A, R, T>;
```

#### `CheckedStatus`

type · `import { CheckedStatus } from '@harness/sdk'`

Status templates checked against a fn's args `A` and result `R`.

```ts
type CheckedStatus<A, R, T extends StatusTemplates> = {
  readonly [Phase in RunPhase]: {
    readonly one: CheckedTemplate<T[Phase]['one'], OneTokens<A, R, Phase>>;
    readonly many: CheckedTemplate<T[Phase]['many'], ManyTokens>;
  };
};
```

#### `CheckedTemplate`

type · `import { CheckedTemplate } from '@harness/sdk'`

A template as written, or an error naming the token it may not use.

```ts
type CheckedTemplate<S extends string, Allowed extends string> =
[Exclude<TokensOf<S>, Allowed>] extends [never] ? S : `Unknown status token {${Exclude<TokensOf<S>, Allowed>}}`;
```

#### `OneTokens`

type · `import { OneTokens } from '@harness/sdk'`

The tokens a phase's `one` form may use.

```ts
type OneTokens<A, R, Phase extends RunPhase> =
| 'fn' | 'args' | `args.${PathsOf<A>}`
| (Phase extends 'done' ? 'output' | `output.${PathsOf<R>}` : never);
```

#### `ManyTokens`

type · `import { ManyTokens } from '@harness/sdk'`

The tokens every `many` form may use.

```ts
type ManyTokens = 'fn' | 'n';
```

#### `PathsOf`

type · `import { PathsOf } from '@harness/sdk'`

The dotted paths into a value (`path`, `range.start`, `items.0`). A value whose keys are open (JSON, records) allows
any path below it; dates and other non-plain objects stop the walk.

```ts
type PathsOf<T, Depth extends readonly unknown[] = []> =
Depth['length'] extends MaxDepth ? never
  : T extends Date | ((...args: never[]) => unknown) ? never
  : T extends readonly (infer Item)[] ? `${number}` | `${number}.${PathsOf<Item, [...Depth, unknown]>}`
  : T extends object
    ? string extends keyof T ? string
    : { [K in keyof T & string]-?: K | `${K}.${PathsOf<NonNullable<T[K]>, [...Depth, unknown]>}` }[keyof T & string]
  : never;
```

#### `TokensOf`

type · `import { TokensOf } from '@harness/sdk'`

The token names a template uses: `'{fn} read {args.path|a file}'` → `'fn' | 'args.path'`.

```ts
type TokensOf<S extends string> = S extends `${string}{${infer Token}}${infer Rest}`
? (Token extends `${infer Name}|${string}` ? Name : Token) | TokensOf<Rest>
: never;
```

## 7. Handlers and progress

### Handlers

A **handler** is a callback a fn asks its *caller* for in the middle of a run ("delete this note?", "pick a folder").
Declare it with `handler({ description, args, returns, required })`. A person answers with a form the chat draws from
the handler's types, an agent or code answers with a function.

- `required: true`: every caller must pass it. A caller that can't answer can't call the fn, and code calling it
  through `ctx.fns` must pass `{ handlers: { … } }` or the call doesn't compile. In the run it is always there.
- `required: false`: the caller may leave it out, so in the run `ctx.handlers.confirm` is `HandlerFn | undefined`, and
  calling it without checking doesn't compile.

A handler that gets no answer (refused, failed, timed out) throws `HandlerFailed`; let it propagate unless the fn can do
something better.

_From `scripts/docs/example/shared/index.ts`:_

```ts
remove: fn({
  description: 'Delete a note.',
  args: g.obj({ id: g.text({ minLength: 1 }) }),
  returns: g.obj({ removed: g.bool() }),
  undo: Before,
  handlers: {
    // optional: a caller that can't answer may still call; the fn must check before calling it
    confirm: handler({ description: 'Delete this note?', args: g.obj({ title: g.text() }), returns: g.bool(), required: false }),
  },
}),
```

_From `scripts/docs/example/server/impl.ts`:_

```ts
remove: {
  async run(args, ctx) {
    const store = deps.open(ctx.dataDir);
    const note = await store.get(args.id);
    if (note === undefined) { return ctx.invalid([{ path: 'id', message: `There is no note ${args.id}.` }]); }
    // the effective setting for the run's person, merged with their groups'
    const editing = await ctx.setting(notes.settings.editing);
    if (editing?.confirmRemove === true) {
      // `confirm` is optional, so the compiler makes the fn check it is there
      if (ctx.handlers.confirm === undefined) { return ctx.fail('caller', 'Removing a note asks first, and this caller cannot answer.', { code: 'denied' }); }
      if (!(await ctx.handlers.confirm({ title: note.title }))) { return ctx.cancelled(); }
    }
    await store.delete(note.id);
    return ctx.done({ removed: true }, { undo: { id: note.id, note } });
  },
  undo: (payload, ctx) => restore(deps.open(ctx.dataDir), payload),
},
```

### Progress

A fn reports progress one of three ways, declared with `progress`:

- nothing (the default, `noProgress`): `ctx.progress` has nothing to call;
- `linear`: a fraction from 0 to 1 with an optional message, `ctx.progress.set(0.4, 'half way')`;
- `steps({ name: 'Title', … })`: named steps, each `pending`, `active`, `done`, `failed` or `skipped` with an optional
  detail, `ctx.progress.step('scan', 'done', '12 notes')`. Only the declared names compile.

Progress reaches the chat live and the fn's renderer gets it typed as `view.progress` (`{ fraction, message? }` or
`{ steps: { <name>: { status, detail? } } }`). Report often enough to look alive but not on every item of a long
loop: each update travels to the hub and on to the chat, so a few dozen updates per run (or one every few hundred ms)
is plenty.

_From `scripts/docs/example/shared/index.ts`:_

```ts
tidy: fn({
  description: 'Merge tags that differ only in case or spaces, across every note.',
  args: g.obj({}),
  returns: g.obj({ merged: g.num({ min: 0, whole: true }) }),
  progress: steps({ scan: 'Read every note', merge: 'Merge the tags', save: 'Save the notes' }),
  // declaring a resume type makes it pausable: it returns this when paused and gets it back on resume
  resume: g.obj({ saved: g.num({ min: 0, whole: true }) }),
}),
```

### Reference: handlers and progress

#### `handler`

function · `import { handler } from '@harness/sdk'`

Declare a handler. People answer handlers with a form; agents and code answer them with a function.

```ts
function handler<A, R, const Req extends boolean>(def: HandlerDef<A, R, Req>): HandlerDef<A, R, Req>;
```

#### `HandlerDef`

interface · `import { HandlerDef } from '@harness/sdk'`

A callback a fn may ask its caller for mid-run ("overwrite?", "pick a folder").

```ts
interface HandlerDef<A, R, Req extends boolean> {
  readonly description: string;
  readonly args: GinType<A>;
  readonly returns: GinType<R>;
  /** A caller that can't answer a required handler can't call the fn. */
  readonly required: Req;
}
```

#### `Handlers`

type · `import { Handlers } from '@harness/sdk'`

A fn's handlers by name.

```ts
type Handlers = Record<string, HandlerDef<unknown, unknown, boolean>>;
```

#### `HandlerFn`

type · `import { HandlerFn } from '@harness/sdk'`

A handler as a function.

```ts
type HandlerFn<HD> = HD extends HandlerDef<infer A, infer R, boolean> ? (args: A) => Promise<R> : never;
```

#### `HandlerArgs`

type · `import { HandlerArgs } from '@harness/sdk'`

The handlers a caller passes: every required one, any optional one.

```ts
type HandlerArgs<H extends Handlers> =
Simplify<{ [K in RequiredKeys<H>]: HandlerFn<H[K]> } & { [K in OptionalHandlerKeys<H>]?: HandlerFn<H[K]> }>;
```

#### `HandlersInRun`

type · `import { HandlersInRun } from '@harness/sdk'`

A fn's own handlers as it sees them: required ones are always there; optional ones may be missing.

```ts
type HandlersInRun<H extends Handlers> =
Simplify<{ readonly [K in RequiredKeys<H>]: HandlerFn<H[K]> } & { readonly [K in OptionalHandlerKeys<H>]: HandlerFn<H[K]> | undefined }>;
```

#### `HasRequiredHandlers`

type · `import { HasRequiredHandlers } from '@harness/sdk'`

Whether a fn has a handler every caller must pass.

```ts
type HasRequiredHandlers<H extends Handlers> = [RequiredKeys<H>] extends [never] ? false : true;
```

#### `linear`

const · `import { linear } from '@harness/sdk'`

Progress as a fraction from 0 to 1: `ctx.progress.set(0.4)`.

```ts
const linear: LinearProgress;
```

#### `steps`

function · `import { steps } from '@harness/sdk'`

Progress as named steps: `steps({ read: 'Read the file', write: 'Write the copy' })`.

```ts
function steps<const S extends Record<string, string>>(steps: S): StepsProgress<S>;
```

#### `noProgress`

const · `import { noProgress } from '@harness/sdk'`

No progress reporting. The default.

```ts
const noProgress: NoProgress;
```

#### `progressDecl`

function · `import { progressDecl } from '@harness/sdk'`

A progress def on the wire.

```ts
function progressDecl(progress: ProgressDef): ProgressDecl;
```

Refers to: [`ProgressDecl`](#core-progressdecl).

#### `ProgressDef`

type · `import { ProgressDef } from '@harness/sdk'`

How a fn reports progress.

```ts
type ProgressDef = NoProgress | LinearProgress | StepsProgress<Record<string, string>>;
```

#### `NoProgress`

interface · `import { NoProgress } from '@harness/sdk'`

```ts
interface NoProgress { readonly type: 'none' }
```

#### `LinearProgress`

interface · `import { LinearProgress } from '@harness/sdk'`

```ts
interface LinearProgress { readonly type: 'linear' }
```

#### `StepsProgress`

interface · `import { StepsProgress } from '@harness/sdk'`

Named steps; the record maps each step to its title.

```ts
interface StepsProgress<S extends Record<string, string>> { readonly type: 'steps'; readonly steps: S }
```

#### `ProgressReporter`

type · `import { ProgressReporter } from '@harness/sdk'`

How a run reports progress, shaped by its fn's progress def. A fn with no progress def gets nothing to call.

```ts
type ProgressReporter<P extends ProgressDef> =
P extends LinearProgress ? { set(fraction: number, message?: string): void } :
P extends StepsProgress<infer S> ? { step(name: keyof S & string, status: StepStatus, detail?: string): void } :
{ readonly type: 'none' };
```

Refers to: [`StepStatus`](#core-stepstatus).

#### `ProgressState`

type · `import { ProgressState } from '@harness/sdk'`

Progress as the UI sees it, shaped the same way: core's `ProgressWire`, with the fn's own step names.

```ts
type ProgressState<P extends ProgressDef> =
P extends LinearProgress ? LinearWire :
P extends StepsProgress<infer S> ? { steps: { [K in keyof S]: StepWire } } :
undefined;
```

## 8. Session state, forms and display

### Session state

Each system has a scratch pad per chat session: values its fns pass to each other without the LLM carrying them (a
working folder, the last search, a selection). A fn declares the fields it uses with `state: g.obj({ … })`, and
`ctx.state` reads and writes only those:

- **Matched by name and type.** Two fns share a field only if both declare that name with the same type. A fn sees just
  its own fields; reading or writing another doesn't compile.
- **Scoped to the system** and to the session (a run outside a session gets state scoped to its turn).
- `get()` returns the current values (a field nothing has set is absent), `set(patch)` writes fields, `clear(...names)`
  removes them, and `update(change)` reads and writes in one step so runs at the same time don't overwrite each other.
- **Undoable and live.** Changes are recorded on the run, so undoing a turn puts state back; renderers see their fn's
  fields as `view.state` and update as they change. Actions declare fields the same way.

_From `scripts/docs/example/server/impl.ts`:_

```ts
find: {
  async run(args, ctx) {
    // `find` declared `state: LastSearch`, so it sees `lastQuery` and nothing else
    const query = args.query ?? (await ctx.state.get()).lastQuery;
    if (query === undefined && args.tag === undefined) {
      // the caller's mistake: an agent gets the problem back and tries again
      return ctx.invalid([{ path: 'query', message: 'Give a query or a tag; there is no earlier search to repeat.' }]);
    }
    const all = await deps.open(ctx.dataDir).all();
    const found: NoteValue[] = [];
    // about twenty updates whatever the size: each one travels to the hub and on to the chat
    const every = Math.max(1, Math.floor(all.length / 20));
    all.forEach((note, index) => {
      if (matches(note, query, args.tag)) { found.push(note); }
      if ((index + 1) % every === 0) { ctx.progress.set((index + 1) / all.length, `${found.length} found`); }
    });
    if (query !== undefined) { await ctx.state.set({ lastQuery: query }); }
    return ctx.done({ notes: found.slice(0, args.limit ?? s.NOTES_FIND_LIMIT), total: found.length });
  },
},
```

### Forms

A fn that declares `form: <gin type>` can wait for its renderer: the run calls `await ctx.form()`, the renderer calls
`view.submit(value)` (checked against the type), and the run carries on with the typed value. The hub stores the
submission if the system is offline meanwhile. A fn can be just a form the person fills in, the agent getting the
answer as the fn's result. `ctx.form` is `undefined` on a fn with no `form`, so waiting on one doesn't compile.

_From `scripts/docs/example/shared/index.ts`:_

```ts
review: fn({
  description: 'Ask the person to approve a note before it is shared. Returns their decision.',
  args: g.obj({ id: g.text({ minLength: 1 }) }),
  returns: Review,
  // the run waits in `await ctx.form()` until the renderer submits this
  form: Review,
  // once answered, the card turns into a plain message
  display: 'message',
}),
```

_From `scripts/docs/example/server/impl.ts`:_

```ts
review: {
  async run(args, ctx) {
    const note = await deps.open(ctx.dataDir).get(args.id);
    if (note === undefined) { return ctx.invalid([{ path: 'id', message: `There is no note ${args.id}.` }]); }
    ctx.attach(notes.attachments.note, note, note.title);
    // waits for the renderer's submit; the hub keeps it if the system is offline meanwhile
    return ctx.done(await ctx.form());
  },
},
```

The renderer is in **The client**.

### Display

`display` decides what stays in the chat once a run is done: `card` (the default: the fn's renderer, or the hub's card),
`message` (the card turns into a plain message; good for forms and questions), or `hidden` (bookkeeping the person
needn't see). While it runs, every run shows its live status.

### Reference: session state, forms and display

#### `SessionState`

interface · `import { SessionState } from '@harness/sdk'`

A system's scratch pad for one session. Each fn, action and renderer sees only the fields it declared, matched by
name and type. Changes are recorded on the run, so undoing a turn puts state back, and they reach renderers live.

```ts
interface SessionState<S extends object> {
  /** The current values. A field nothing has set yet is absent. */
  get(): Promise<Readonly<Partial<S>>>;
  set(patch: Partial<S>): Promise<void>;
  /** Read, change and write in one step, so concurrent runs don't overwrite each other. */
  update(change: (current: Readonly<Partial<S>>) => Partial<S>): Promise<void>;
  /** Remove fields. */
  clear(...fields: Array<keyof S & string>): Promise<void>;
}
```

## 9. Outcomes: done, invalid input, failed, paused, cancelled, undo

Every run ends in an **outcome**, built with the ctx helpers, which are typed to the fn (its result, undo and resume
types). Return exactly one; never throw to report something the caller should hear.

| Helper | Status | Use it when |
|---|---|---|
| `ctx.done(result, { undo?, cost? })` | `done` | it worked. `undo` records how to reverse it (undoable fns only) |
| `ctx.invalid(problems, { cost? })` | `invalid-input` | **the caller's mistake** the types can't catch: an id that doesn't exist, an end before a start, nothing to search for. Give a message or `[{ path, message }]` problems (`path` a dotted path into the args, `''` for the whole). An agent gets the problems back and retries; the chat shows the run softly as "Retried". Not an error |
| `ctx.fail(class, message, { code?, data?, undo?, cost? })` | `failed` | **a real failure.** `class` is `retry` (temporary: the hub retries per `limits.retries`), `caller` (refused for a reason the caller must hear: full, denied, conflict) or `fatal` (a bug or broken setup). `code` is a stable word for programs (`not-found`, `denied`, `full`) |
| `ctx.pause(state)` | `paused` | it can stop and continue later (fns with a `resume` type only) |
| `ctx.cancelled({ partial?, undo? })` | `cancelled` | it stopped because it was cancelled or the person said no |

**Invalid input versus errors.** Args that don't fit the fn's gin types never reach the fn: the hub and the runtime
answer `invalid-input` for bad JSON, a schema mismatch and failed validation. Inside the fn, use `ctx.invalid` for any
other mistake in the args, and `ctx.fail` only for real failures. A thrown exception becomes a `failed` outcome with the
error's message; that is for bugs, not for expected cases.

**Cancel and timeouts.** `ctx.signal` aborts when the run is cancelled, paused or past its deadline. Pass it to anything
that takes a signal (fetch, child processes, ayepi calls) and check `ctx.signal.aborted` in long loops; then return
`ctx.cancelled({ partial })` with what you managed. A run past its deadline is reported as timed out whatever it returns.
`ctx.allow({ cancel: false })` narrows what can be done to the run right now ("no cancelling during the commit").

### Undo

Declaring `undo: <gin type>` makes a fn undoable: `ctx.done(result, { undo: payload })` records the payload, and the
server **must** implement `undo(payload, ctx)`, which returns an `UndoOutcome`: `{ status: 'undone', summary }`,
`{ status: 'failed', error }`, or `{ status: 'gone' }` when there is nothing left to undo. Record the payload with
`ctx.undoWith(payload)` as soon as the first irreversible step is done, so a run that then fails or is cancelled can
still be undone (the last payload recorded wins). The payload holds what is needed to put things back (the previous
content by `ContentRef`, the row as it was), never a reference to something that can change. Undo runs later, perhaps
after a restart, with only the payload and an `UndoCtx`.

_From `scripts/docs/example/server/impl.ts`:_

```ts
/** Put a note back as it was before a change: delete it if it didn't exist, else restore it. */
async function restore(store: NoteStore, before: BeforeValue): Promise<UndoOutcome> {
  if (before.note === null) {
    return (await store.delete(before.id)) ? { status: 'undone', summary: `Removed note ${before.id} again.` } : { status: 'gone' };
  }
  await store.put(before.note);
  return { status: 'undone', summary: `Restored "${before.note.title}".` };
}
```

### Pause and resume

Declaring `resume: <gin type>` makes a fn pausable. Register `ctx.onPause(() => state)`: when someone pauses the run, the
hub asks this hook for the state to resume from, the run settles as `paused` straight away, `ctx.signal` aborts, and any
call the fn is waiting on rejects with `Suspended` (let it propagate; never catch it). Resuming runs the fn again with
`ctx.resume` set: `ctx.resume.state` is the state, `ctx.resume.calls` the results of child calls that finished (by call
key, so they aren't made again), and `ctx.resume.reason` says whether a person resumed it or the hub restarted.

_From `scripts/docs/example/server/impl.ts`:_

```ts
tidy: {
  async run(_args, ctx) {
    const store = deps.open(ctx.dataDir);
    // a resumed run starts where it paused
    let saved = ctx.resume?.state.saved ?? 0;
    // asked to pause: say where to resume from; the run settles as paused and `ctx.signal` aborts
    ctx.onPause(() => ({ saved }));
    ctx.progress.step('scan', 'active');
    const all = await store.all();
    ctx.progress.step('scan', 'done', `${all.length} notes`);
    ctx.progress.step('merge', 'active');
    const changed = all.flatMap((note) => {
      const tags = cleanTags(note.tags);
      return tags.join('\n') === note.tags.join('\n') ? [] : [{ ...note, tags, updatedAt: deps.now() }];
    });
    ctx.progress.step('merge', 'done', `${changed.length} to change`);
    ctx.progress.step('save', 'active');
    for (const note of changed.slice(saved)) {
      if (ctx.signal.aborted) { return ctx.cancelled({ partial: { merged: saved } }); }
      await store.put(note);
      saved += 1;
    }
    ctx.progress.step('save', 'done');
    return ctx.done({ merged: changed.length });
  },
},
```

**Results with rows.** A fn whose result is rows gives the rows to the person (an attachment holding a
`ResultTable`) and the model a `ResultBlock`: `resultBlock(table, { fn: { name, signature }, args })` from
`@harness/sdk/server` says what the person sees and that the model shouldn't repeat it, lists the columns, the row
count, the total when counted, whether there are more rows with the exact call for the next page (`nextPage`), the
sort, and a `sample` of the first rows within `HARNESS_RESULT_SAMPLE_TOKENS` (long text cut at
`HARNESS_RESULT_CELL_CHARS`), with a `sampleNote` saying how much is there. `ResultBlockType` is its gin form for
`returns`.

### Reference: outcomes: done, invalid input, failed, paused, cancelled, undo

#### `ResultBlock`

const, type · `import { ResultBlock } from '@harness/sdk'`

```ts
  shown: string;
  columns: {
    name: string;
    nullable: boolean;
    kind: "number" | "boolean" | "date" | "text" | "integer" | "datetime" | "time" | "uuid" | "json" | "binary";
  }[];
  rowCount: number;
  truncated: boolean;
  sort: {
    column: string;
    dir: "asc" | "desc";
  }[];
  sample: Record<string, z.core.util.JSONType>[];
  fn: {
    name: string;
    signature: string;
  };
  total?: number | undefined;
  nextPage?: {

    limit: number;
```

#### `Outcome`

type · `import { Outcome } from '@harness/sdk'`

How a run ended. Build it with the ctx helpers (`ctx.done(result)`, `ctx.invalid(problems)`, `ctx.fail(...)`,
`ctx.pause(state)`, `ctx.cancelled()`), which are typed to the fn. Its wire form is core's `OutcomeWire`.
`invalid-input` is the caller's mistake (an agent retries it, the chat shows it softly); `failed` is a real error.

```ts
type Outcome<T extends FnTypes> =
| { status: 'done'; result: T['result']; undo?: T['undo']; cost?: Usd }
| { status: 'invalid-input'; invalid: InvalidInput; cost?: Usd }
| { status: 'paused'; resume: T['resume']; cost?: Usd }
| { status: 'failed'; error: FnError; undo?: T['undo']; cost?: Usd }
| { status: 'cancelled'; partial?: T['result']; undo?: T['undo']; cost?: Usd };
```

Refers to: [`FnError`](#core-fnerror), [`InvalidInput`](#core-invalidinput), [`Usd`](#core-usd).

```ts
}
```

## 10. Kinds and agents

### Kinds

A **kind** decides what a fn's definition looks like and how it runs. A native fn's author declares everything because
the code is the definition; a fn of a kind carries only a **payload** (the kind's definition data), and the kind works
out the rest from it. Declare a kind with `kind({ description, applicability, payload, approveChanges?, status? })`:
`applicability` tells an agent when to pick this kind for a new fn, `payload` is its gin type, `approveChanges`
(default `true`) makes declaring or changing a fn of the kind at runtime wait for approval, and `status` gives the
words of its fns that declare none (they may use `{fn}`, `{n}`, `{args.…}` and `{output.…}`).

_From `scripts/docs/example/shared/index.ts`:_

```ts
kinds: {
  stamp: kind({
    description: 'A fn that adds a note with a fixed title and tags; its args are the text.',
    applicability: 'Use for quick capture: a journal, an inbox, a standing list.',
    payload: Stamp,
    // declaring or changing a stamp fn at runtime needs no approval: it can only add notes
    approveChanges: false,
  }),
},
```

The server implements each kind with three functions, and fns of the kind are **not** implemented one by one:

- `check(payload, ctx)`: problems beyond the payload's type (an unknown fn, a program that doesn't compile), as
  sentences; `[]` when it is fine.
- `describe(payload, ctx)`: everything else about a fn with this payload, as a `KindDescription`: its `args` and
  `returns` (gin definitions, made with `wireType(type)`), the fns it calls (`deps`), the named types and resources it
  refers to, its operations, and optionally handlers, progress, status words, limits and display. `ctx.type(name)` and
  `ctx.fn(name)` look up other declarations while describing.
- `run(payload, args, ctx)`: run one fn of the kind. The args arrive as JSON the hub checked against `describe`'s args;
  read them with the same gin type (`parseValue`). The result is JSON (`encodeValue`). `ctx.call(name, args)` calls the
  deps `describe` returned, by name; `ctx.handler(name, args)` asks a handler; `ctx.progress(...)` reports either shape.
- `undo(payload, undo, ctx)`, optional, for kinds whose fns are undoable.

_From `scripts/docs/example/server/impl.ts`:_

```ts
kinds: {
  stamp: {
    // problems beyond the payload's type
    check: (payload) => Promise.resolve(payload.tags.filter((tag) => tag !== tag.toLowerCase()).map((tag) => `Tag "${tag}" must be lowercase.`)),
    // everything else about a stamp fn, worked out from its payload
    describe: () => Promise.resolve({
      args: wireType(StampArgs),
      returns: wireType(Note.shape),
      deps: [],
      operations: { cancel: true, pause: false, undo: false, background: true },
    }),
    async run(payload, args, ctx) {
      // a kind's args arrive as JSON the hub checked against `describe`; read them with the same gin type
      const input = parseValue(StampArgs, args);
      const note = await create(deps.open(ctx.dataDir), ctx.config, { title: payload.title, text: input.text, tags: payload.tags });
      if (note === undefined) { return ctx.fail('caller', full(ctx.config), { code: 'full' }); }
      return ctx.done(encodeValue(Note.shape, note));
    },
  },
},
```

Fns of a kind are declared with the kind's ref, `kinds.<kind>.fn({ description, payload, args?, returns?, … })`, by
its own system or by any other. Give `args` and `returns` to type the ref; the hub checks them against what `describe`
derives. Without them the ref takes and returns `Json`. The fn runs on the system that defines its kind.

### Agents

An **agent** is a fn of the hub's `llm` kind: an LLM with instructions, a model, tools and context, callable like any
fn. `agent({ description, args, returns, instructions, … })` declares one; any system can ship its own (a reviewer, a
planner) with tools from any system.

- `instructions` is a Handlebars template; `{{args.question}}` reads the args.
- `returns: g.text()` gives a chat-style answer; any other type asks the model for a structured answer of that type.
- `tools` are fn refs it can use; `canLoad` lets it add more while running (`{ fns?, systems?, tags? }`, default
  `'none'`); `maxTools` (default 32) caps the toolset.
- `context` is context it always has; `hooks` run on each user message it gets.
- `model` picks a model (`{ model: 'openrouter/…' }`, `{ category: 'fast' }` or `{ auto: { cost, speed, accuracy } }`);
  without one the hub's default is used.
- `mode` and `contextor` choose how fns and context reach the model (strict tools or the static toolset; plain,
  suggest or graph context), `maxOutput` caps one answer, `maxIterations` (default 16) caps the steps,
  `toolExecution` is `parallel` (default) or `sequential`.

_From `scripts/docs/example/shared/index.ts`:_

```ts
.with(({ fns, context: ctx }) => ({
  fns: {
    // an agent: a fn of the hub's `llm` kind with instructions, tools and context
    librarian: agent({
      description: 'Answers questions from the notes, quoting their titles.',
      args: g.obj({ question: g.text() }),
      returns: g.text(),
      instructions: 'Answer {{args.question}} from the notes. Quote the titles you used. Say so when no note covers it.',
      tools: [fns.find, hub.fns.ask_user],
      context: [ctx.guide],
    }),
    // calls other fns through its deps, by ref: its own system's `review` and the hub's `ask_user`
    publish: fn({
      description: 'Share a note once the person approves it.',
      args: g.obj({ id: g.text({ minLength: 1 }) }),
      returns: g.obj({ published: g.bool(), note: g.optional(g.text()) }),
      deps: { review: fns.review, ask: hub.fns.ask_user },
    }),
  },
}));
```

### Reference: kinds and agents

#### `kind`

function · `import { kind } from '@harness/sdk'`

Declare a kind.

```ts
function kind<P>(def: { description: string; applicability: string; payload: GinType<P>; approveChanges?: boolean; status?: StatusTemplates; }): KindDef<P>;
```

#### `KindDef`

interface · `import { KindDef } from '@harness/sdk'`

A fn kind as declared in a contract: what its payload looks like and when to use it.

```ts
interface KindDef<P> {
  readonly description: string;
  /** When an agent should pick this kind for a new fn. */
  readonly applicability: string;
  readonly payload: GinType<P>;
  /** Whether declaring or changing a fn of this kind at runtime needs approval. */
  readonly approveChanges: boolean;
  /** The status words of its fns that declare none. They may use `{fn}`, `{n}` and `{args}` / `{output}` paths. */
  readonly status: StatusTemplates | undefined;
}
```

#### `KindImpl`

interface · `import { KindImpl } from '@harness/sdk'`

A kind's implementation. The payload is typed; a fn's args and result aren't (they depend on the payload), so they
arrive as JSON the hub has already checked against `describe`'s types.

```ts
interface KindImpl<P, Cfg = unknown> {
  /** Problems with a payload beyond its type (an unknown fn, a program that doesn't type-check). */
  check(payload: P, ctx: KindCheckCtx): Promise<string[]>;
  describe(payload: P, ctx: KindCheckCtx): Promise<KindDescription>;
  run(payload: P, args: Json, ctx: KindRunCtx<Cfg>): Promise<Outcome<UntypedFn>>;
  undo?(payload: P, undo: Json, ctx: UndoCtx<Cfg>): Promise<UndoOutcome>;
  /** What a run of a payload's fn would do, for a fn `describe` gave an `approval` with a preview (a write query). */
  preview?(payload: P, args: Json, ctx: PreviewCtx<Cfg>): Promise<PreviewAnswer>;
}
```

Refers to: [`Json`](#core-json), [`KindDescription`](#core-kinddescription), [`PreviewAnswer`](#core-previewanswer), [`UndoOutcome`](#core-undooutcome).

#### `KindCheckCtx`

interface · `import { KindCheckCtx } from '@harness/sdk'`

What a kind can look up while checking a payload.

```ts
interface KindCheckCtx {
  type(name: QualifiedName): Promise<GinTypeDef | undefined>;
  fn(name: QualifiedName): Promise<{ args: GinTypeDef; returns: GinTypeDef; description: string } | undefined>;
}
```

Refers to: [`GinTypeDef`](#core-gintypedef), [`QualifiedName`](#core-qualifiedname).

#### `KindRunCtx`

interface · `import { KindRunCtx } from '@harness/sdk'`

A kind's run context: like `FnCtx`, but calls are by name (limited to the deps `describe` returned).

```ts
interface KindRunCtx<Cfg = unknown> extends Omit<FnCtx<UntypedFn, Cfg>, 'fns' | 'handlers' | 'progress'> {
  call(fn: QualifiedName, args: Json, options?: { key?: string; background?: boolean }): FnCall<Json>;
  handler(name: string, args: Json): Promise<Json>;
  progress(state: { fraction: number; message?: string } | { step: string; status: StepStatus; detail?: string }): void;
}
```

Refers to: [`Json`](#core-json), [`QualifiedName`](#core-qualifiedname), [`StepStatus`](#core-stepstatus).

#### `KindRef`

interface · `import { KindRef } from '@harness/sdk'`

A typed handle to a fn kind. `kinds.api.fn({...})` declares a fn of that kind.

```ts
interface KindRef<N extends QualifiedName, P> {
  readonly ref: 'kind';
  readonly name: N;
  readonly payload: GinType<P>;
  /**
   * Declare a fn of this kind. Only the payload is needed; the kind derives the rest. Give `args`/`returns` to type
   * the ref (the hub checks them against what the kind derives); without them the ref takes and returns JSON.
   */
  fn<A = Json, R = Json, const T extends StatusTemplates = StatusTemplates>(def: KindFnInput<P, A, R, T>): FnDef<KindFnTypes<KindRef<N, P>, P, A, R>>;
}
```

Refers to: [`Json`](#core-json), [`QualifiedName`](#core-qualifiedname).

#### `AnyKindRef`

type · `import { AnyKindRef } from '@harness/sdk'`

```ts
type AnyKindRef = KindRef<QualifiedName, unknown>;
```

Refers to: [`QualifiedName`](#core-qualifiedname).

#### `kindFn`

function · `import { kindFn } from '@harness/sdk'`

The fn def of a kind fn, built by `KindRef.fn`. The kind's `describe` fills in the rest when the hub imports it.

```ts
function kindFn<N extends `${string}/${string}`, P, A, R, const T extends StatusTemplates = { active: { one: string; many: string; }; retry: { one: string; many: string; }; done: { one: string; many: string; }; failed: { one: string; many: string; }; }>(kind: KindRef<N, P>, def: KindFnInput<P, A, R, T>): FnDef<KindFnTypes<KindRef<N, P>, P, A, R>>;
```

#### `KindFnInput`

interface · `import { KindFnInput } from '@harness/sdk'`

What `kinds.x.fn()` takes. Only the payload is required: everything else a fn has is worked out by the kind's
`describe`. `args`/`returns` are optional typing hints: when given, the hub checks them against what the kind
derived, and the ref is typed by them (otherwise by `Json`).

```ts
interface KindFnInput<P, A, R, T extends StatusTemplates = StatusTemplates> {
  description: string;
  instructions?: string;
  payload: P;
  args?: GinType<A>;
  returns?: GinType<R>;
  /** What its runs say they are doing; the kind's words when absent. Tokens are checked against `args` and `returns`. */
  status?: StatusInput<NoInfer<A>, NoInfer<R>, T>;
  visibility?: Audience;
  tags?: string[];
  /** Example calls, of the `args` and `returns` types when given (JSON otherwise). */
  examples?: FnExampleOf<NoInfer<A>, NoInfer<R>>[];
  /** For a fleet: always one instance (a saved query of one database), or whichever is first available. */
  routing?: FnRouting;
  /** Checks its system runs before a run starts and after it is done (an agent's answer checked, retried with why). */
  validate?: FnValidators<NoInfer<A>, NoInfer<R>, {}>;
  /**
   * What it does to data: its mode (`read`, `write`, `delete`), the named types it reads and writes, and whether its own
   * work is free (reading a file). The hub works out the rest and folds in what it uses (`FnFacts`).
   */
  effects?: FnEffects;
  /** How many of its examples go into the model's prompt with it (the hub's default when absent); the rest page with `hub/fn_examples`. */
  promptExamples?: number;
}
```

Refers to: [`Audience`](#core-audience), [`FnEffects`](#core-fneffects), [`FnRouting`](#core-fnrouting).

#### `KindFnTypes`

interface · `import { KindFnTypes } from '@harness/sdk'`

The types of a fn of some kind. Its deps, handlers and progress come from the kind at runtime.

```ts
interface KindFnTypes<K extends AnyKindRef, P, A, R> {
  kind: K; payload: P; args: A; result: R; deps: {}; handlers: {}; progress: NoProgress; resume: never; undo: never; state: {}; form: never;
}
```

#### `UntypedFn`

interface · `import { UntypedFn } from '@harness/sdk'`

The types of a fn known only at runtime (what a kind's implementation runs).

```ts
interface UntypedFn {
  kind: AnyKindRef; payload: unknown; args: Json; result: Json; deps: {}; handlers: Handlers; progress: ProgressDef; resume: Json; undo: Json; state: Record<string, Json>; form: Json;
}
```

Refers to: [`Json`](#core-json).

#### `agent`

function · `import { agent } from '@harness/sdk'`

Declare an agent: an LLM with instructions, tools and context, callable like any fn. A system can ship its own
agents (a code reviewer, a planner) with tools from any system.

```ts
function agent<A, R, const T extends StatusTemplates = { active: { one: string; many: string; }; retry: { one: string; many: string; }; done: { one: string; many: string; }; failed: { one: string; many: string; }; }>(def: AgentInput<A, R, T>): FnDef<AgentFnTypes<A, R>>;
```

#### `AgentInput`

interface · `import { AgentInput } from '@harness/sdk'`

What `agent()` takes.

```ts
interface AgentInput<A, R, T extends StatusTemplates = StatusTemplates> {
  description: string;
  args: GinType<A>;
  /** Text for a chat-style agent; any type for a structured answer. */
  returns: GinType<R>;
  /**
   * The agent's own prompt (Handlebars; `{{args.x}}` reads the args): its job, how to use its tools step by step,
   * what to answer with, and worked examples. Write it for the model.
   */
  instructions: string;
  /** What callers read (the fn's `instructions`): when to use it, how to call it, what it returns, pitfalls. */
  usage?: string;
  model?: ModelChoice;
  tools?: AnyFnRef[];
  /** Fns the agent may add to its toolset while running. Default `none`. */
  canLoad?: { fns?: AnyFnRef[]; systems?: string[]; tags?: string[] } | 'none';
  /** Default 32. */
  maxTools?: number;
  context?: AnyContextRef[];
  hooks?: AnyHookRef[];
  /** Default `parallel`. */
  toolExecution?: 'parallel' | 'sequential';
  /** Default 16. */
  maxIterations?: number;
  /** How fns reach the model: strict tools (the hub's default) or the static toolset. */
  mode?: NonNullable<LlmPayload['mode']>;
  /** What goes into each prompt: plain (the hub's default), suggest, or graph (for small local models). */
  contextor?: NonNullable<LlmPayload['contextor']>;
  /** The most tokens one answer may take; the model's own limit when absent. */
  maxOutput?: number;
  visibility?: Audience;
  tags?: string[];
  /** Example calls (args of its own args type), shown to other agents on its card. */
  examples?: FnExampleOf<NoInfer<A>, NoInfer<R>>[];
  /** Checks its system runs on the args before it starts and on its answer after (`retry` sends the model back with why). */
  validate?: FnValidators<NoInfer<A>, NoInfer<R>, {}>;
  /** What its runs say they are doing; the hub's agent words when absent. Tokens are checked against `args` and `returns`. */
  status?: StatusInput<NoInfer<A>, NoInfer<R>, T>;
}
```

Refers to: [`Audience`](#core-audience), [`ModelChoice`](#core-modelchoice).

#### `AgentFnTypes`

type · `import { AgentFnTypes } from '@harness/sdk'`

The types of an agent fn.

```ts
type AgentFnTypes<A, R> = KindFnTypes<LlmKind, LlmPayload, A, R>;
```

#### `LlmPayload`

type · `import { LlmPayload } from '@harness/sdk'`

The payload of a `hub/llm` fn, as stored.

```ts
type LlmPayload = Infer<typeof LlmPayloadType>;
```

#### `LlmKind`

type · `import { LlmKind } from '@harness/sdk'`

The ref type of the hub's `llm` kind.

```ts
type LlmKind = typeof hub.kinds.llm;
```

## 11. Settings and attachments

### Settings

A **setting** is a typed control an admin (or, if allowed, the person or a fn) sets per actor: a user, a group, an
agent, a system or a hub MCP. `setting({ description, type, appliesTo, merge, editableBy? })`:

- `type`: the value's gin type (the admin's form is drawn from it).
- `appliesTo`: the actor kinds it can be set on (`user`, `group`, `agent`, `system`, `mcp`).
- `merge`: how a user's value combines with their groups': `user-wins`, `union`, `intersect` or `most-restrictive`.
- `editableBy`: besides admins, `self` (the person for themselves) and `fns` (fns may set it).

A run reads the effective value for its person with `await ctx.setting(notes.settings.editing)`, typed, or `undefined`
when nothing is set; always handle `undefined` with a safe default.

_From `scripts/docs/example/shared/index.ts`:_

```ts
settings: {
  editing: setting({
    description: 'Whether removing a note asks first.',
    type: g.obj({ confirmRemove: g.bool() }),
    appliesTo: ['user', 'group'],
    merge: 'most-restrictive',
  }),
},
```

### Attachments

An **attachment** is data a fn adds to the assistant message its run belongs to, drawn by the system's renderer for
it (a diff, a chart, a card). Declare it with `attachment({ description, data })`; a run attaches with
`ctx.attach(notes.attachments.note, data, title)`, and the data is checked against the type (wrong data doesn't
compile). Without a renderer the chat shows the data as a plain card. To attach a link instead (a resource, a URL, a
stored file or another run), use `ctx.link({ resource })`, `{ url, title }`, `{ file, title }` or `{ run, title }`.

### Reference: settings and attachments

#### `setting`


Refers to: [`ActorKind`](#core-actorkind), [`SettingEditor`](#core-settingeditor), [`SettingMerge`](#core-settingmerge).

## 12. Context and commands

**Context** is text for the LLM: a guide, a skill, a reference, the state of something. The hub suggests it when a
message is about it, an agent can always have it (`alwaysFor`, `agent({ context })`), and a person loads it with a
`/command`. `context({ description, … })`:

- **Static** when it has `content`: the text is in the contract. `{{name}}` in the content is filled from the
  command's args.
- **Dynamic** when it has no `content`: the server makes the text each time it is loaded, so it can be current (a folder
  tree, the newest notes). The server **must** implement it under `context`, as `async (args, ctx) => text`.
- `command: { name, args? }` makes it a `/name` command. Each arg is `arg(hint, { required? })`, required unless
  `{ required: false }`; the implementation's `args` are typed from them (`{ tag?: string }`).
- `fns`: fns that come with it, loaded into the toolset when it is loaded; `suggestFns`: fns suggested with it.
- `imports`: other context it brings along.
- `suggest` and `visible`: who it may be suggested to and who can find it in searches (`both`, `user`, `llm`, `none`).
- `alwaysFor`: agents that always have it loaded. `tags`: free labels.
- For the graph contextor (the one small local models use): `weight` (0 to 2, default 1) is how much it counts,
  `summary` a short form sent when the budget allows only a little, `parent` and `next` place it in a hierarchy or a
  chain of pieces, and `links` are weighted edges (`{ to, relation, weight? }`) to other context, fns, named types or
  resources (`{ uri, section? }`). `imports` and `brings` make items travel together; `suggests`, `related`, `about`,
  `parent` and `next` pass on part of an item's score.

_From `scripts/docs/example/shared/index.ts`:_

```ts
context: {
  guide: context({
    description: 'How to keep notes here: search before adding, short titles, lowercase tags.',
    content: 'Search with notes/find before adding a note, so you do not add one twice. Keep titles short and tags lowercase.',
    fns: [fns.find, fns.add],
    // for the graph contextor: how much it counts, a short form for tight budgets, and what it relates to
    weight: 1.2,
    summary: 'Search before adding; short titles; lowercase tags.',
    links: [{ to: types.Note, relation: 'about' }, { to: fns.tidy, relation: 'suggests', weight: 0.3 }],
  }),
  // no `content`: dynamic, so the server makes the text; the command is `/notes [tag]`
  recent: context({
    description: 'The newest notes, optionally with one tag.',
    command: { name: 'notes', args: { tag: arg('Only notes with this tag', { required: false }) } },
  }),
},
```

_From `scripts/docs/example/server/impl.ts`:_

```ts
context: {
  recent: async (args, ctx) => {
    const all = await deps.open(ctx.dataDir).all();
    const shown = all.filter((note) => args.tag === undefined || note.tags.includes(args.tag.toLowerCase())).slice(0, s.NOTES_FIND_LIMIT);
    if (shown.length === 0) { return args.tag === undefined ? 'There are no notes yet.' : `No note has the tag "${args.tag}".`; }
    return shown.map(markdownOf).join('\n\n---\n\n');
  },
},
```

A dynamic context's `ctx` is the system context (config, LLM, resources, types, log, data folders) plus who is asking
(`ctx.user`, `ctx.time`, and the session when there is one). Keep context focused: a short guide that names its fns
beats a long manual, and the hub budgets what it sends to small models.

### Reference: context and commands

#### `context`

function · `import { context } from '@harness/sdk'`

Declare static context.

```ts
/** Declare static context. */
function context<const Args extends Record<string, ArgDef<boolean>> = {}>(def: ContextInput<Args> & { content: string; }): ContextDef<ArgsOf<Args>, false>;
/** Declare dynamic context: the server implements it. */
function context<const Args extends Record<string, ArgDef<boolean>> = {}>(def: ContextInput<Args>): ContextDef<ArgsOf<Args>, true>;
```

#### `ContextInput`

interface · `import { ContextInput } from '@harness/sdk'`

What `context()` takes.

```ts
interface ContextInput<Args extends Record<string, ArgDef<boolean>>> {
  description: string;
  /** Makes it a `/name` command. Args fill `{{name}}` in the content (or are passed to a dynamic implementation). */
  command?: { name: string; args?: Args };
  imports?: AnyContextRef[];
  fns?: AnyFnRef[];
  suggestFns?: AnyFnRef[];
  suggest?: Audience;
  visible?: Audience;
  alwaysFor?: AnyFnRef[];
  tags?: string[];
  /** How much it counts when scored, from 0 (left out) to 2. Default 1. */
  weight?: number;
  /** A short form shown when the budget allows only a little of it. */
  summary?: string;
  parent?: AnyContextRef;
  next?: AnyContextRef;
  /** Weighted links to other context, fns, types and resources. */
  links?: ContextLinkDef[];
}
```

Refers to: [`Audience`](#core-audience).

#### `ContextDef`

interface · `import { ContextDef } from '@harness/sdk'`

Content for the LLM. Static content is written in the contract; dynamic content is produced by the system.

```ts
interface ContextDef<A, Dynamic extends boolean> {
  readonly description: string;
  readonly dynamic: Dynamic;
  readonly content: string | undefined;
  readonly command: { readonly name: string; readonly args: Readonly<Record<string, ArgDef<boolean>>> } | undefined;
  readonly imports: readonly AnyContextRef[];
  readonly fns: readonly AnyFnRef[];
  readonly suggestFns: readonly AnyFnRef[];
  /** Who it may be suggested to. Default `both`. */
  readonly suggest: Audience;
  /** Who can find it in searches. Default `both`. */
  readonly visible: Audience;
  /** Always loaded for these agents. */
  readonly alwaysFor: readonly AnyFnRef[];
  readonly tags: readonly string[];
  /** How much it counts when the graph contextor scores it: 0 leaves it out, 2 doubles it. Default 1. */
  readonly weight: number | undefined;
  /** A short form shown when the budget allows only a little of it; its description otherwise. */
  readonly summary: string | undefined;
  /** Its parent in a hierarchy (a guide over its chapters). */
  readonly parent: AnyContextRef | undefined;
  /** The next piece of a chain (a long text in ordered pieces). */
  readonly next: AnyContextRef | undefined;
  readonly links: readonly ContextLinkDef[];
  /** Phantom: the command's args. Never set at runtime. */
  readonly argsType?: A;
}
```

Refers to: [`Audience`](#core-audience).

#### `AnyContextDef`

type · `import { AnyContextDef } from '@harness/sdk'`



interface · `import { ContextLinkDef } from '@harness/sdk'`


type · `import { ContextLinkTarget } from '@harness/sdk'`

## 13. Hooks

A **hook** adds information to each user message before the LLM sees it (the git branch, the pinned notes, the open
ticket). Declare it with `hook({ description, order?, timeout? })`: lower `order` runs first (default 100), and after
`timeout` ms (default 1000) the message goes on without it, so keep hooks fast.

The server implements it as `async (input, ctx) => contributions`. `input` is the message (`HookInput`: session, turn,
text, user, time); each contribution is one of:

- `{ type: 'text', tag, text }`: a tagged block the LLM can tell apart from the person's words;
- `{ type: 'suggest-context', items: [{ context, why? }] }` and `{ type: 'suggest-fns', items: [{ fn, why? }] }`:
  suggestions, by ref;
- `{ type: 'load', context?, fns? }`: load these now.

Return `[]` when there is nothing to add.

_From `scripts/docs/example/shared/index.ts`:_

```ts
hooks: { pinned: hook({ description: 'Adds the titles of pinned notes to each message.', order: 60, timeout: 300 }) },
```

_From `scripts/docs/example/server/impl.ts`:_

```ts
hooks: {
  pinned: async (input, ctx) => {
    const pinned = (await deps.open(ctx.dataDir).all()).filter((note) => note.pinned).slice(0, s.NOTES_HOOK_TITLES);
    const blocks: HookContribution[] = [];
    if (pinned.length > 0) { blocks.push({ type: 'text', tag: 'pinned-notes', text: `Pinned notes: ${pinned.map((note) => `"${note.title}"`).join(', ')}.` }); }
    if (/\bnotes?\b/i.test(input.text)) { blocks.push({ type: 'suggest-context', items: [{ context: notes.context.guide, why: 'The message is about notes.' }] }); }
    return blocks;
  },
},
```

### Reference: hooks

#### `hook`

function · `import { hook } from '@harness/sdk'`

Declare a hook. Defaults: order 100, timeout 1000 ms.

```ts

```

Refers to: [`Duration`](#core-duration).

#### `HookDef`

interface · `import { HookDef } from '@harness/sdk'`

```ts

```

Refers to: [`HookBlock`](#core-hookblock).

interface · `import { HookRef } from '@harness/sdk'`
